# Restriction register

This register lists the limits found in Portcove and what should happen to each.
It records the location, scope and class of each limit, its rationale, what a
player or pipeline sees at the limit, which callers depend on it, a proposed
disposition and the validation evidence. It serves the arbitrary-limits work
under #1422. Each mechanism pull request changes code and adds its own
`## Restriction register rows`. This register is the shared inventory that
those rows update. It changes no product behavior.

"Restriction" here means any limit on content, size, history, time or workflow.
It is unrelated to the catalog's typed acquisition restrictions (download holds)
in [CATALOG.md](CATALOG.md).

Rows were traced at commit `47f824704`, re-pinned to `e53fee315` (`main` after
#8618 and #8622), and refreshed at `4d04ccba8` after the merged #8619, #8626, #8630,
#8631, #8632, #8633, #8634, #8635, #8636, #8637, #8638, #8640, #8641, #8642,
#8644 and #8661. Locations cite each limit by file and by the name of the
constant, function or literal that holds it. Other cells may still quote a line
number; those refer to `47f824704`. A row is evidence about that commit, not a
live status. Mechanism pull requests own the changes and their acceptance.

## How to read this register

Owner direction: "Library growth, provider growth, accumulated history, and
temporary resource pressure must not make supported work permanently unusable."
Every retained limit needs a concrete purpose and one of these classes:

| Class   | Meaning                                                                          | What the row must name                                                                              |
| ------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 1       | Actual external provider, runtime, filesystem or format constraint               | The provider, OS or format fact. An application's own format or compatibility choice is not class 1 |
| 2       | Integrity, identity, ownership or containment requirement                        | The property protected                                                                              |
| 3       | Resource budget for a bounded unit of work                                       | The unit and its working continuation                                                               |
| 4       | Accidental ceiling on supported content, library size, history or workflow scope | Removed by the owning mechanism                                                                     |
| UNKNOWN | Not established                                                                  | The measurement or decision that settles it                                                         |

"A page size, cache capacity, or processing batch with functioning continuation
is not automatically a product defect. Follow its actual callers and failure
behavior." A continuation counts only when a production caller persists and
consumes it. A budget whose continuation no production caller consumes is an
effective ceiling and is class 4. Its rationale starts with "Effective ceiling".

### Sessions

The Session column names the open pull request whose diff changes a line that
contains the row's cited name.

| Session value                     | Meaning                                                                  |
| --------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `#NNNN`                           | That pull request changes the cited limit                                |
| `#NNNN (area; limit not changed)` | A class 1–3 row in that pull request's area that it does not change      |
| `unassigned`                      | No open pull request changes the limit                                   |
| `unassigned (file also in #NNNN)` | No owner, but that pull request changes the same file for another reason |

`(merged)` marks a pull request that has landed. A row it changed was refreshed
against `main`. PH-01 is assigned to #8631, which added a retention route that is
built off. The pull requests and their diffs were read on 2026-11-23 and refreshed
on 2026-11-24:

| ------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------- | ------ |

| #8631        | Listing-body window with a listing-floor ledger (built off) | Merged |
| #8632        | Catalog size ceilings                                       | Merged |
| #8633        | Scan coverage and store-owned-folder protection             | Merged |
| #8635        | Catalog identity indexes and workspace observations         | Merged |

All listed pull requests have landed. Later changes keep the register current
through the [ratchet](#ratchet). Each mechanism pull request confirms its own rows.

### Method

The sweep covered Core (`crates/portcove-core`), the CLI, Tauri
(`apps/desktop/src-tauri`), React (`apps/desktop/src`),
`portcove-release-tools`, `portcove-listing-carrier` and `scripts/`. A pattern
sweep of production code (test modules and test files excluded) found 1,303
candidate lines:

- named `MAX`, `LIMIT`, `BOUND`, `CAP` and `BUDGET` constants;
- `.take(N)` and `.slice(0, N)` calls;
- SQL `LIMIT`;
- `per_page` and page sizes;
- `Duration` literals and timeout fields;
- retry and attempt counts.

Each area was then traced by hand from producer to every consumer. That tracing
covered literal comparisons that refuse, truncate or stop paging. It also
covered archive envelopes and the non-numeric restrictions the owner named:

- guide length;
- title-specific branches;
- enumerated release qualifications;
- save-format and per-engine capability;
- optional evidence that became a universal gate;
- repeated whole-inventory work.

A pattern hit is a candidate, not a finding. Section B records limits whose
behavior was traced. [Appendix F](#f-other-named-bounds) classifies every other
named production bound constant more briefly.

When more than one area reached the same site, the rows were merged into one:

| Retired Id | Merged into     |
| ---------- | --------------- |
| DL-04      | PV-21           |
| CD-08      | DL-08           |
| CD-09      | DL-11           |
| DK-11      | LC-11           |
| SC-29      | LC-14 and LC-16 |
| SC-30      | LC-15           |
| AR-13      | MD-02           |
| MD-03      | AR-12           |
| PV-19      | MD-15           |

Retired Ids are not reused. AR-05 was split into AR-05 (Windows filesystem
facts, class 1) and AR-15 (path and link containment, class 2).

### Ratchet

`scripts/check-document-links.test.mts` enforces the register (added in
#8650):

- Every cited file must exist.
- Each name cited after a file must still occur in it as a whole word.
- Every production bound constant must be named in section B or appendix F, or
  in the test's short list of reviewed exclusions. That covers Rust constants and
  statics with a `MAX`, `MAXIMUM`, `LIMIT`, `BOUND`, `CAP` or `BUDGET` name
  segment, and top-level TypeScript constants named like a bound (`maxItems`,
  `maximumBytes`, `requestLimit`, `pageBound`, `readCeiling`) in `crates/*/src`,
  `apps/desktop/src`, `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src` and all of `scripts/`.

Changes to those sources select the test, so a new or removed bound constant
needs a register edit in the same change. [QUALITY](QUALITY.md) states the
contract.

## A. Summary

Section B holds 250 traced rows. Of these, 77 class-4 rows and 26 UNKNOWN rows
have no open pull request. Appendix F has 425 rows of further named bounds.

| Area                      | Class 1 | Class 2 | Class 3 | Class 4 | UNKNOWN | Rows    |
| ------------------------- | ------- | ------- | ------- | ------- | ------- | ------- |
| Lifecycle                 | 0       | 1       | 16      | 12      | 2       | 31      |
| Mods and recipes          | 0       | 1       | 11      | 4       | 3       | 19      |
| Providers                 | 2       | 1       | 12      | 4       | 3       | 22      |
| Scans                     | 0       | 2       | 17      | 10      | 2       | 31      |
| Deadlines and retry       | 1       | 1       | 21      | 8       | 0       | 31      |
| Publication history       | 1       | 0       | 4       | 5       | 1       | 11      |
| Catalog distribution      | 0       | 0       | 2       | 13      | 1       | 16      |
| Discovery and maintenance | 4       | 1       | 13      | 12      | 5       | 35      |
| Desktop and IPC           | 0       | 0       | 14      | 9       | 2       | 25      |
| Archive safety            | 1       | 3       | 6       | 0       | 6       | 16      |
| Semantic restrictions     | 0       | 4       | 0       | 8       | 1       | 13      |
| **Total**                 | **9**   | **14**  | **116** | **85**  | **26**  | **250** |

Rows by session: unassigned 162, #8634 24, #8633 14, #8637 11, #8641 9, #8640 8, #8638 5, #8632 5, #8619 3, #8661 3, #8630 3, #8635 1, #8631 1, #8626 1.

Appendix F by class: class 1 25, class 2 146, class 3 112, class 4 121, class UNKNOWN 21.

**Resolved by #8633.** SC-16 recorded that incomplete store detection could
permit a Move out of a store-owned folder. Store ownership now reports
`Undetermined` when any store read is incomplete, and the Move is refused.
The Windows registry subkey count limit was removed.

## B. Register

Columns: Id; location (file and the name that holds the limit); what it bounds
and its scope; class; rationale; what happens at the limit; the production
consumers traced; the proposed disposition; existing test evidence ("none" when
no test exercises the boundary); and the session.

### Expensive whole-inventory work in the register's areas

- **Lifecycle:**
  - `metadata_snapshot` serializes every profile revision, retained history,
    membership and artwork record. It runs on each metadata export, library move
    plan and recovery-bundle plan, and twice per bundle creation.
  - `add_games_to_library` rescans pending setups once per request.
- **Scans:**
  - SC-08: pruning re-checks the same oldest 20,000 observations after every
    scan.
  - SC-17: full store detection runs on every move check.
  - SC-22: the selected original folder is walked again on every preparation.
- **Providers:** GitLab resolution reads the whole release history each time
  (PV-09). Each "show older" step in the release chooser re-reads from page 1
  (PV-08).
- **Catalog distribution:** definition acquisition downloads every content
  target on each refresh, with no digest reuse (DL-08). The definition snapshot
  is serialized whole into one SQLite column (CD-05).
- **Deadlines:** the browsing game-file refresh hashes every registered source
  in one 30 s batch (DL-21).
- **Publication history:** each publication-inventory reader restarts its scans
  (PH-05). History verification reads every retained listing body.

## D. Unassigned work

The work below has no open pull request. Each item names its rows and is scoped
to start one session. Items are ranked by player and pipeline impact. Section A
gives the counts.

1. **Library transfer, retained installs and other lifecycle ceilings (LC-02–LC-10, LC-12–LC-17, LC-24, LC-25, LC-27, LC-30–LC-33).**
   - Library metadata: #8641 removed the profile, retained-reference and membership counts and gave export, import and recovery bundles a shared admission budget of 1/32 of physical memory, with a 256 MiB fallback. The single-document format can still refuse a growing library, including one with accumulated setup revisions, or an import on a machine with less memory (LC-02–LC-06, LC-09, LC-12, LC-30). #8645 is open and proposes a separate chunked document format as a follow-up.
   - Retained builds: #8641 automatically retires older unpinned offers. The installs stay on disk (LC-08). #8645 tracks retirement-record cleanup and documents the proposed follow-up for removing an eligible individual install.
   - Recovery creation: parent-directory and staging-folder counts can refuse recovery work (LC-07). Player-data backup and migration can still refuse at `MAX_MATCHING_FILES` (LC-31).
   - Host preferences: the 64 KiB shared document caps favorites, collections and DAT references across libraries and can block unrelated preference writes (LC-13).
   - Portable saves: the folder history cap has no continuation (LC-14). Decode deferral is lost between CLI runs (LC-15). Large save sets export but fail to import (LC-17).
   - Batches: Add to Library and install batches refuse more than 512 games without a production caller that chunks (LC-10, LC-32). Pending setup records need manual forgetting (LC-33). Older unfinished batches drop out of view (LC-27).
   - Scope: chunked transfer with resumable export and import, individual install cleanup with recovery safeguards, and Core-side continuation for the remaining lifecycle ceilings.
2. **Guide-length bound blocks registration routes (SR-01, SR-02, SR-03, DM-30).**
   - Where: Core caps steps plus references at 8 and warnings at 8 (`executable_contract_is_valid` in `catalog.rs`, and `catalog/listings.rs`). `catalog-authoring.mts` (`checkSetupGuide`) and `catalog-route-facts.mts` mirror the cap.
   - Effect: 24 ports and 46 listings are already at 8. 94 authored listings could not take the two generated steps, so their otherwise usable routes are withheld as `guide-bound`.
   - Scope: replace the count with a byte envelope per guide, or a collapsible guide structure that released readers accept. Update every producer and consumer together.
3. **Portable saves and title-specific qualifications (SR-04, SR-05, SR-07, SR-08, SR-10).**
   - SR-04: portable saves exist only for Shipwright 9.3.1, so they block after any Shipwright update and every other port is unqualified.
   - SR-05: Ghostship legacy recovery needs active 3.0.0 exactly.
   - SR-07: Doom 3 Linux asset hints are compiled into Rust.
   - SR-08: OpenMoHAA prefixes are compiled into Rust.
   - Scope: catalog-declared save-format and capability facts with generic adapters. Keep the integrity rules: only a verified unchanged save format moves, and qualified backups keep their seal checks.
4. **2 GiB file ceilings (SC-01, SC-20, SC-31, SC-22, SC-23).**
   - Effect: scans and selected-disc inspection skip or refuse supported images over 2 GiB, such as the Ace Combat 6 Xbox 360 image (7,835,492,352 B).
   - Exact-member intake: it refuses when unrelated files sit in the folder (SC-22). The 2 GiB directory-set total is 97.5% used by Doom 3 RoE (SC-23).
   - Scope: profile-declared image bounds and member-directed lookup.
   - Also: SC-08 (pruning never reaches stale rows), SC-10 (future-only enrollment above about 100k files), SC-07 (non-Unicode names) and SC-28 (`MAXIMUM_SHAPE_PATHS`, mirrored by DM-40).
5. **Desktop and IPC ceilings (DK-02–DK-05, DK-07, DK-08, DK-21–DK-23).**
   - Browsing projection: past its ceilings it shows "unavailable" filters for the whole library (DK-02). Retained facts get 250 ms in a fixed order (DK-03).
   - Game-file responses are refused past 4 MiB (DK-04).
   - Activity history cannot be paged (DK-05).
   - Artwork stores need manual repair (DK-07). The Steam artwork file fails every operation past about 3,100 records (DK-08).
   - SteamGridDB reports page 40 as the last page (DK-21).
   - Artwork choices past 8,192 fail export, move and recovery bundles (DK-22).
   - No-argument inspection refuses past 4,096 ports (DK-23).
   - Scope: paged reads with cursors, consistent bounds with eviction, and truthful incompleteness.
6. **Mods and recipes after #8640 (MD-08, MD-10, MD-15, LC-28).** #8640
   resolved the count ceilings (MD-02, MD-06, MD-07, MD-11, MD-12, MD-16, LC-30,
   PV-24) under one 4,096-package setup capacity. What remains:
   - MD-08: upstream-valid Recomp mods are refused for text length.
   - MD-10: Daggerfall Unity mods are limited to 32 MiB and 128 objects each.
   - MD-15: GitHub mod sources read only the newest 30 releases.
   - LC-28: preserved copies use a linear probe capped at 1,024.
   - The owner still has to decide whether the setup capacity itself is a
     supported ceiling.
   - Scope: streamed inspection with byte budgets, and paging.
7. **Deadlines that scale with catalog or library size (DL-06, DL-08, DL-09, DL-10, DL-12, DL-21, DL-22, DL-26).**
   - DL-08: definition acquisition fetches every target within 120 s.
   - DL-09: 20 s total per target, including the body.
   - DL-10: 25 s for the listing document.
   - DL-12: 20 s for a 16 MiB catalog.
   - DL-21: browsing refresh hashes every source within 30 s, with no partial result.
   - #8634 does not change these files.
   - Scope: progress-based idle bounds, digest reuse and resumable fetches.
8. **Provider and artwork enumeration outside #8637 (PV-02, PV-03, PV-08, DL-19, DK-21).**
   - PV-02: one 60 s deadline covers the whole GitHub release walk.
   - PV-03: saved-package checks refuse past 10 inventories on every check.
   - PV-08: the chooser stops at 1,000 releases and re-reads from page 1.
   - DL-19: SteamGridDB listings above 1,000 images stay Unknown.
   - Scope: persisted cursors and request budgets with continuation.
9. **Catalog growth ceilings outside #8632 (CD-03, CD-04, CD-05, CD-10, DM-01, DM-29, DM-31, DM-42).**
   - CD-03: the 16 MiB signed catalog.
   - CD-04: the legacy 4 MiB envelope kept for old clients.
   - CD-05: the whole definition snapshot is one 16 MiB column.
   - CD-10: 10,000 external runtime registrations.
   - DM-01: 32 assets and 4 releases per listing observation.
   - DM-42: release site file ceilings, which stop publication. #8632 removes the public site's entry count (DM-28); DM-29's silent truncation of withdrawn identities remains.
   - DM-31: 10,000 catalog records.
   - Scope: a coherent growth contract with CAT, sharded outputs and per-definition rows.
10. **Discovery and maintenance stalls (DM-02, DM-08, DM-11, DM-13, DM-18, DM-21, DM-22, DM-23, DM-25, DM-37, DM-43).**
    - DM-13: the observer checkpoint is one 32 MiB document.
    - DM-02: facts reproduction reads one release page.
    - DM-08: a capped discovery slice never completes.
    - DM-23: relocation probes always skip the same repositories past 64.
    - DM-25: the intake status read fails above about 3,900 comments.
    - Scope: per-source records, persisted cursors and fair rotation with a reported remainder.
11. **Publication history ceilings outside #8626 and #8631 (PH-02–PH-06).**
    - The history segment chain, the floor and active-file counts, and the application-update TUF records are lifetime ceilings.
    - PH-05: #8626 removes the list ceilings but states that the 160-request admission budget is not resolved.
    - Scope: bounded rebase or retention with verified floors.
12. **Measurements needed before a disposition.** Every UNKNOWN row names its measurement. The archive envelopes against real packages come first:
    - AR-06: any non-ASCII entry name in catalog assets.
    - AR-08: block layout of the 17 `.tar.xz` assets.
    - AR-12: the largest supported mod packs.
    - AR-16 and AR-17: the largest disc filesystem and STFS package.

**Unassigned expensive whole-inventory work.** These rows describe repeated
work, not a ceiling, and are recorded so a session can take them:

- DK-01: every workspace read rebuilds the whole state and rehashes the catalog
  of about 7 MB. Reads poll every 1–3 s while work runs, and every mutation
  forces a full read.
- DK-16: React looks up ports with `.find` inside per-item loops.
- DM-10: the discovery state is stringified on every commit.
- DM-18: the maintenance report is one psql JSON output.
- DM-27: the whole issue and PR history is read every run.
- Lifecycle `metadata_snapshot`: it serializes every revision, and runs twice
  per bundle creation.

**Class-4 constants in appendix F.** These were classified briefly rather than
traced end to end. Promote them to section B when a session takes them up. The
ones that act in the shipped application:

- `MAX_REGISTRY_SUBKEYS` and `MAX_REGISTRY_VALUE_BYTES`: one oversized
  third-party Uninstall entry fails the whole inventory, so an installed
  application is treated as unregistered.
- `MAX_PROVIDER_RECORDS_PER_ASSET`: artwork attributions are silently cut to 64.
- `MAX_PATH_BYTES` and `MAX_PATH_DEPTH` (`portable_tree.rs`): these are not OS
  limits, but one long path refuses a whole library transfer or recovery bundle.
- `MANIFEST_LIMIT` (`service/archive_data_transition.rs`): the data transition
  reads an installation manifest with a bound that its writer does not apply.
- `MAX_RECORD_BYTES` (`install_batch.rs`): it is not derived from the item cap,
  so a batch update can fail partway through.

**Appendix F bounds at or near their limit.** These are mostly in repository and
release tooling, but each can stop a pipeline:

- `maximumFiles` (`scripts/required-node-test-reporter.mts`, 512): the
  repository has 509 `*.test.mts` files.
- `maximumProductionControllerWorkflowBytes` (96 KiB): the production
  application-update workflow is 91,598 bytes (93%).
- `semanticSourceBlobLimit` (16 MiB): already exceeded once; a 21 MB
  qualification record needed a pinned exception.
- `ELEMENT_WORKING_BUDGET` (`listing_delivery.rs`): caps delivery at about
  10,300 listings, and its own comment says larger directories need a
  continuation that does not exist.
- `archiveLimit` (`scripts/maintenance-recovery-artifact.mts`, 128 MiB): the
  backup producer does not check it, so an oversized backup fails the scheduled
  job instead of deferring.
- `MAX_THUMBNAIL_BYTES` (1 MiB): a photographic 1280×720 PNG derivative may
  exceed it, leaving no derivative. Not measured.

## E. Retained limits

These 124 limits are proposed for retention. Each is class 1, 2 or 3. A
class 1–3 row whose disposition is "replace", "needs measurement" or a
conditional retain stays only in section B until its session decides.

Continuation: `carried` queue with receipts in the worker state, resumed by the next derive                                                                                           | Carried entries reported unfinished; later batches land them without a person                                                                                                                                                                            |

The code comment's "over 3,080 commits" is the historical distance of the first Windows preview baseline, not current headroom. |
| PV-01 | 3     | Forecast budget and notes window; selection walks `next_page` lazily with no page cap (release.rs:926-960)                                                                                                                                       | Forecast undercounts long walks; notes past 1,000 releases report `InstalledNotListed` with a releases-page link                                                                                                                                         |
| PV-05 | 1     | GitHub caps a release at 1,000 assets                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Complete inventory always reached                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| PV-06 | 2     | Bounds checksum authority ambiguity for one asset                                                                                                                                                                                                | Verification refusal `checksum_source_limit`                                                                                                                                                                                                             |

Today max 181 Windows slots                                                                                                                                                                         | Oldest entry evicted; CI test fails asking to raise constant                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| PV-09 | 3     | Resolved by #8637: lists are read by exact or filtered lookup, or walked to the end with a persisted continuation that later checks resume.                                                                                                      | A walk cut short is a typed temporary incomplete outcome, never absence.                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| PV-10 | 3     | Resolved by #8637: exact or filtered lookup, or a complete walk with persisted continuation.                                                                                                                                                     | A walk cut short is temporary and incomplete, never absence.                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| PV-11 | 3     | Resolved by #8637: the count was removed; the concurrency bound limits parallel requests, not results.                                                                                                                                           | No refusal at a count.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| PV-12 | 3     | Resolved by #8637: read to the end at GitLab's documented page size.                                                                                                                                                                             | No refusal at a count.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| PV-13 | 3     | #8637: a presentation window. The selected release is read exactly, the channel choice reads older history as far as it needs, and `release_history_complete` says when older releases exist.                                                    | Older releases are reported as existing; the chooser does not claim completeness.                                                                                                                                                                        |
| PV-14 | 3     | Resolved by #8637: links are read to the end.                                                                                                                                                                                                    | No refusal at a count.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| PV-15 | 3     | Unit = one catalog read; `next_page` continuation                                                                                                                                                                                                | Partial catalog labelled; next call continues                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| PV-17 | 3     | Resolved by #8637: a community of any size is read as a digest-verified chunk snapshot; the per-response bounds are decompression guards.                                                                                                        | A cut-short read is temporary and incomplete.                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| PV-18 | 3     | Resolved by #8637: the version count was removed; the stores are caches whose overflow only re-reads chunks after an interruption.                                                                                                               | No package files dropped at a count.                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| PV-21 | 1     | Provider rate-limit responses                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | Network error with `retry_after_seconds`; cached data served when available                                                                                                                                                                              |

| PV-23 | 3     | Resolved by #8637: pages follow the provider, with one lookahead row deciding "more results".                                                                                                                                                    | Later maps remain reachable.                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| PV-24 | 3     | Resolved by #8640: the closure is resolved completely with no size of its own; only the setup capacity applies.                                                                                                                                  | A closure larger than one setup can hold is refused.                                                                                                                                                                                                     |

Release manifest fixture checks the exact size and fallback.                                                                                                                                                           | Scan bounds require an explicit choice. Oversized manifests keep ordinary suggestions.                                                                                                                                                                   |

## F. Other named bounds

Every other production constant whose name has a `MAX`, `MAXIMUM`, `LIMIT`,
`BOUND`, `CAP` or `BUDGET` segment is listed here, with a brief classification.
Promote a constant to section B when a session takes it up or its class is 4 or
UNKNOWN and it needs tracing.

| Constant                                   | File                                                                              | Value and scope                                                                                       | Class   | Reason                                                                                                                                   | At the limit                                                                    | Disposition                                                                        |
| ------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `MAX_PREFERENCE_BYTES`                     | `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/application_update.rs`                                | 4 KiB; read of the self-written update-preferences file                                               | 2       | Containment: bounded read of a non-authoritative two-field file Portcove writes                                                          | Defaults used (Beta, automatic checks); no error shown                          | Retain                                                                             |
| `MAX_CLI_BYTES`                            | `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/cli_context.rs`                                       | 256 MiB; streaming SHA-256 identity of the standalone CLI executable                                  | 2       | Executable-trust containment: bounded hashing and marker scan of a discovered binary                                                     | CLI candidate rejected as not a bounded regular file                            | Retain                                                                             |
| `MAX_PROGRESS_BYTES`                       | `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/lib.rs`                                               | 16 KiB; one relayed launch-progress event file from the supervisor                                    | 2       | Containment: bounded read of a small transient IPC JSON event                                                                            | Event skipped ("nothing new"); progress is best-effort                          | Retain                                                                             |
| `MAX_PORT_ID_LENGTH`                       | `apps/desktop/src-tauri/src/recent_play_shortcuts.rs`                             | 128 bytes; `--portcove-play <port-id>` startup argument                                               | 2       | Identity: mirrors Core listing id bound (`listings.rs` `id.len() > 128`); argument grants nothing                                        | Argument ignored                                                                | Retain; consider sharing Core's constant                                           |

### Release scan checkpoint retention

| Bound                              | Source                                   | Value and scope                                  | Class | Rationale                                                                                                                           | Behavior at the limit                                                                                        | Disposition                                                                              |
| ---------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------ | ----- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `archiveLimit`                     | `scripts/release-content-checkpoint.mts` | 8 MiB ZIP for one scan checkpoint                | 2     | Matches the existing bounded Actions artifact downloader.                                                                           | Restore refuses unbound or oversized archive metadata before transfer.                                       | Retain                                                                                   |
| `recentRunLimit`, `recentRunPages` | `scripts/release-content-checkpoint.mts` | 20 scheduled runs per page, up to eight pages    | 3     | Pages cover 40 days on the four-per-day schedule. A validated older successful run can establish expiry after that window.          | Invalid or incomplete pages refuse recovery.                                                                 | Validate alongside schedule or retention changes                                         |
| `priorAttemptLimit`                | `scripts/release-content-checkpoint.mts` | 20 exact earlier-attempt lookups per restoration | 4     | Effective ceiling for a long streak of reruns. Attempts can replace the visible conclusion for an older successful run.             | Restoration pauses rather than guessing which attempt succeeded.                                             | Measure repeated exhaustion and use a complete provider attempt inventory when available |
| `retentionMs`                      | `scripts/release-content-checkpoint.mts` | 30 days from producer completion                 | 3     | Matches the configured artifact retention. A verified expiry permits a fresh bounded scan.                                          | A missing in-window artifact stops restoration.                                                              | Keep aligned with the hosted workflow                                                    |
| `retainedCheckpointLimit`          | `scripts/release-content-scan.mts`       | 8 MiB less 64 KiB container overhead             | 3     | Compact retained JSON fits the existing transfer envelope. Per-key continuation records the measured bytes needed for a later scan. | Oversized records stay in the current report. Subsequent transfers for that key defer until the record fits. | Retain existing evidence and track storage holds                                         |
